## 5. Subsetting recipe (one-time author-side; NOT a build step)

Per Resolved Decision #15 + planner Q4, the woff2 is generated locally
and committed. Re-run only when upstream Fraunces revs.

```bash
# Prerequisite: pip install fonttools brotli
# Source: https://fonts.google.com/specimen/Fraunces (download family);
# locate Fraunces[opsz,SOFT,WONK,wght].ttf in the unzipped archive.

pyftsubset Fraunces[opsz,SOFT,WONK,wght].ttf \
  --output-file=apps/frontend/public/fonts/Fraunces-subset.woff2 \
  --unicodes='U+0000-007F,U+00A0-00FF,U+2000-206F' \
  --layout-features='kern,liga,smcp,onum,ital' \
  --flavor=woff2 \
  --no-ignore-missing-unicodes \
  --desubroutinize
```

Coverage:
- `U+0000-007F` — Latin Basic (ASCII).
- `U+00A0-00FF` — Latin-1 Supplement (em-dash, en-dash, accented
  chars, copyright, etc.).
- `U+2000-206F` — General Punctuation (en/em-quad, en/em-space,
  hair-space, prime / double-prime, ellipsis).
- `--layout-features=kern,liga,smcp,onum,ital`:
  - `kern` — pair kerning. Required for editorial text rhythm.
  - `liga` — common ligatures (`fi`, `fl`).
  - `smcp` — small caps. Used by the M5 chapter-break label
    ("SESSION RESUMED"). Required.
  - `onum` — old-style numerals. Used by the empty-pane preface +
    future timestamp prose. Required for the editorial mood.
  - `ital` — italic axis. The empty-pane preface + future skim
    chapter-break label render in italic. **Required.**

Expected output size: ~75–85 KB woff2 (within the spec's ~80 KB
target). If the output exceeds 100 KB, the developer narrows the
unicode-range or drops `smcp` (the Phase 5 spec only uses small
caps for the M5 chapter-break label; it could be replaced by CSS
`text-transform: uppercase` + a tighter `letter-spacing` if needed,
though the small-caps glyphs read better).

The subset file is regenerated only when Fraunces upstream changes
(approximately yearly per Google Fonts cadence). **No CI build
step**. Reversibility test: delete `Fraunces-subset.woff2` and
verify the page renders in Charter / Georgia at ~99% size with no
layout shift.

## 6. Cascade impact

The cascade order (per existing `global.css` header) is:
**`reset.css → tokens.css → global.css → feature-local sibling
sheets`**. M2a does NOT change the order.

Where M2a's diff lands:

- `tokens.css`:
  - **Add** the canonical color tokens (`--color-ink`,
    `--color-ink-muted`, etc.) with oklch values for light + dark.
  - **Add** the four alias declarations
    (`--color-text: var(--color-ink); …`) right after the canonical
    declarations so the alias is in the same `:root` block.
  - **Revise** existing color tokens whose name persists to use the
    new oklch values.
  - **Add** the typography tokens (3 stacks + 6 scale steps + 2
    leading + 1 measure).
  - **Revise** existing `--text-*` literals to spec values
    (`0.72rem → 0.75rem`, etc.).
  - **Add** the motion tokens (4 durations + 3 easing).
  - **Add** the noise-overlay token.
  - **Add** the `@supports not (color: oklch(0% 0 0))` block with
    hex fallbacks (re-using the existing 24 hex literals — no new
    literals added).
  - **Add** two `@font-face` blocks (regular + italic).

- `global.css`:
  - **Add** one rule:
    `.session-pane { background-image: var(--noise-overlay-light); }`
    — though this could equally land in `SessionView.css`. The
    designer's recommendation: **land it in `SessionView.css`** so
    the noise-overlay rule lives with the surface it decorates,
    keeping `global.css` lean. Either works; the developer picks.
  - **Add** the dark-mode suppression rule:
    `@media (prefers-color-scheme: dark) { .session-pane { background-image: none; } }`.
  - **Add** the reduced-motion suppression rule:
    `@media (prefers-reduced-motion: reduce) { .session-pane { background-image: none; } }`
    — note this is a SEPARATE rule from the existing global zero-out
    (which targets `animation-duration` / `transition-duration` /
    `scroll-behavior` only — `background-image` is NOT in the
    existing zero-out's property list).

- `features/sessions/SessionsTable.css`:
  - **Edit** one line in the `tr[data-deep-link="true"]` rule:
    `animation: deep-link-pulse 600ms ease-out 1;` becomes
    `animation: deep-link-pulse var(--motion-pulse) var(--ease-out) 1;`.
    The `1` iteration count stays a literal (it is not a token; it
    is the algorithmic count of iterations).
  - The `@keyframes deep-link-pulse` block itself does NOT change —
    its `0%` peak (`color-mix(... var(--color-accent) 22% ...)`) and
    `100%` resting (`color-mix(... var(--color-accent) 8% ...)`)
    already consume `var(--color-accent)`, so the new oklch sienna
    retints the keyframe automatically.

The 11 feature-local CSS consumers continue to read alias names
unchanged; they are not in M2a's diff.

## 7. WCAG AA contrast table

The full table lives at `wcag.md`. The summary here calls out the
load-bearing pairs that drive the M2a acceptance gate. All ratios are
designer-measured against the oklch CIE2000 conversions; the developer
**must** re-measure against the implementation tokens with the
M6 contrast script (the Phase 4 baseline pattern) before the M2a
review round closes — codex precedent at Phase 4 M6 caught the Toast
border at <3:1 even after the designer reported it passing.

| Pair                                                    | Light  | Dark   | Verdict       |
|---------------------------------------------------------|--------|--------|---------------|
| `--color-ink` on `--color-surface`                      | 14.1:1 | 14.6:1 | AAA · normal  |
| `--color-ink-muted` on `--color-surface`                | 5.7:1  | 7.2:1  | AA · normal   |
| `--color-ink` on `--color-surface-raised`               | 13.4:1 | 12.9:1 | AAA · normal  |
| `--color-ink` on row hover (4% ink)                     | 13.6:1 | 14.0:1 | AAA · normal  |
| `--color-ink` on row selected (8% accent)               | 12.8:1 | 12.6:1 | AAA · normal  |
| `--color-ink` on deep-link pulse peak (22% accent)      | 9.8:1  | 9.4:1  | AAA · normal  |
| `--color-warn` on `--color-surface`                     | 4.9:1  | 5.4:1  | AA · normal   |
| `--color-warn` on row selected tint                     | 4.6:1  | 5.1:1  | AA · normal   |
| `--color-accent` 100% on `--color-surface`              | 4.5:1  | 4.7:1  | AA · UI 1.4.11|
| up-to-date pill (success 70/15)                         | 5.2:1  | 6.1:1  | AA · normal   |
| not-stored pill (accent 70/15)                          | 5.0:1  | 5.6:1  | AA · normal   |
| outdated pill (warn 70/15)                              | 4.7:1  | 5.5:1  | AA · normal   |
| source-missing pill (error 70/15)                       | 5.0:1  | 5.8:1  | AA · normal   |
| Toast.success border on raised                          | 3.4:1  | 3.8:1  | SC 1.4.11     |
| Toast.error border on raised                            | 3.6:1  | 3.7:1  | SC 1.4.11     |
| Toast.info border on raised (uses `--color-accent`)     | 3.1:1  | 3.4:1  | SC 1.4.11     |
| `--color-border-strong` on `--color-surface`            | 3.2:1  | 3.0:1  | SC 1.4.11     |
| `--color-border` on `--color-surface` (decorative)      | 1.7:1  | 1.8:1  | not WCAG-gated |

The Toast.info border on raised (3.1 : 1 light) is the tightest
WCAG-gated pair in the table. **Reviewer-flagged risk**: oklch
`color-mix` interpolation can drift slightly from sRGB-anchored
expectations; the developer's actual measurement may land at
2.95–3.10 : 1. If the measurement falls below 3:1, the mitigation is
to bump the `--color-accent` 65% mix on `.toast.info` to 70% (a
one-line edit in `Toast.css`) or, alternatively, to anchor the toast
borders against `--color-surface-raised` directly with a precomputed
oklch value rather than the color-mix recipe. **The designer
recommends the developer measure with the Phase 4 M6 contrast script
before assuming the table holds.** Codex precedent.

The hairline `--color-border` is intentionally below 3:1 — it is a
decorative separator, not a UI border per SC 1.4.11. The
sticky-bar top edge uses `--color-border-strong` instead specifically
because it must clear 3:1 (the sticky bar carries the M1b's selection
count + Pagination, and the top edge demarcates the chrome from the
scrollable list above).

## 8. Reading wash (noise overlay) — signature detail #6

### Visual rationale

The reading wash is the chunk's most subjective design choice. At 3%
opacity the SVG fractalNoise is **invisible at a glance**. It only
reads as warmth — the kind a printed page has and a screen page
typically doesn't. The opacity choice (3%, not 5% or 8%) is calibrated
so:

- On `oklch(98% 0.01 70)` light surface, the wash is just-detectable
  with deliberate squinting; reads as paper grain.
- On `oklch(20% 0.02 70)` ink (body text), the wash does NOT intrude
  on contrast — the WCAG measurement above (14.1:1 for ink-on-
  surface) holds within rounding because the noise's per-pixel
  alpha never exceeds ~0.03 against the `--color-surface` baseline.
- On the Toast surface (which sits on `--color-surface-raised`,
  not `--color-surface`), the wash does NOT apply — only
  `.session-pane` carries the rule. Toast contrast is unaffected.

### Light-mode-only justification

In dark mode `--color-surface` is `oklch(15% 0.01 70)` — already a
deep warm ink. Adding a noise overlay would muddy it (the noise
introduces a small luminance scatter that competes with the dark
warmth). The dark surface already carries the editorial mood without
texture; the wash is decorative-only on light.

### Reduced-motion suppression rationale

The wash is purely decorative — it carries no signal. Users with
`prefers-reduced-motion: reduce` are typically also light-sensitive
(per the WCAG WAI guidance on motion = "any visual change that is not
essential"). Suppressing the noise under reduced-motion respects that
setting even though the wash does not animate.

### Rule (final form)

```css
/* In SessionView.css (recommended) OR global.css. Designer
 * recommends SessionView.css so the rule lives with the surface. */
.session-pane {
  background-image: var(--noise-overlay-light);
  background-repeat: repeat;
}

@media (prefers-color-scheme: dark) {
  .session-pane {
    background-image: none;
  }
}

@media (prefers-reduced-motion: reduce) {
  .session-pane {
    background-image: none;
  }
}
```

**Important**: the existing M1a global reduced-motion zero-out rule
(`global.css` lines 145-154) targets `animation-duration` /
`transition-duration` / `scroll-behavior` only — `background-image`
is NOT in its property list. The dedicated `background-image: none`
rule above is therefore additive, not redundant. The developer
confirms this against `global.css` head comment when landing the
chunk.

## 9. Decisions & tradeoffs

### 9.1 Option B aliasing is the correct call

Option A (rewrite all 11 feature-local CSS files to use the new
`--color-ink` / `--color-surface` names) would have been **cleaner**
in the long run, but each rewrite introduces a code-review surface
where a typo could land an unintended visual regression. The
backwards-compat aliases (Option B) cost **4 declarations**
(`--color-text: var(--color-ink); --color-text-muted: var(--color-ink-muted); --color-bg: var(--color-surface); --font-sans: var(--font-chrome);`)
and let M2a land token-only with **zero feature-local CSS rewrites**.
The aliases survive indefinitely; M2b/M3/M4/M5 may incrementally
migrate consumers to the canonical names if a future planner
prefers, but no planner needs to. **Recommendation: keep aliases
permanent.** The cost is 4 declarations; the benefit is a clean
M2a diff with one risk surface (tokens.css) instead of twelve.

### 9.2 Empty-pane preface promoted to display italic NOW

Spec line 36 lists "empty-pane preface" as a display-font surface.
The preface is rendered by `SessionView.tsx` (M1a-delivered);
promoting it to `--font-display` italic is a **two-line CSS edit**
in `SessionView.css` (the existing `.empty-prose-1` rule):

```css
.empty-prose-1 {
  font-family: var(--font-display);  /* was implicitly --font-sans */
  font-style: italic;                 /* was upright */
  font-size: var(--text-lg);
  /* … */
}
.empty-mark {
  font-family: var(--font-display);  /* was implicitly --font-sans */
  font-style: italic;                 /* was upright */
  font-size: var(--text-2xl);         /* was --text-xl */
  /* … */
}
```

The `--text-xl → --text-2xl` shift on `.empty-mark` is also
designer-recommended (the spec calls for the empty-pane preface at
the display layer; bumping the glyph to `--text-2xl` matches the
spec's rendering intent for a "centered small illustration" per spec
line 595). **No structural React change**; just two CSS rules.

If the developer prefers to defer this until M2b's SessionView
rewire (which has more visual surface to coordinate), it's a clean
deferral — the chunk still lands the typography tokens that M2b will
consume. **Recommendation: do it in M2a.** The visible delight
(reading "Select a session…" in italic Fraunces against the warm-paper
surface with the noise wash) is a satisfying moment the user will see
before any other Archive-room aesthetic surface lands.

### 9.3 Hex fallback aesthetic regression is acceptable

The 24 existing hex literals predate the Archive-room aesthetic.
Rendering on a browser without `oklch()` support (Chromium <111,
Firefox <113, Safari <15.4) shows the **Phase 4 cool-blue palette**,
not the Archive-room warm-paper palette. The fallback's
`--color-accent` (`#2864d4`) is blue, not sienna; the deep-link pulse
in fallback mode glows blue instead of amber.

Two options were considered:

- **Option A (chosen)**: keep the existing 24 hex literals as the
  fallback; document the regression. The fallback aesthetic is
  cohesive (Phase 4 was a competent design); only the hue differs.
  Hex isolation invariant holds at exactly 24, no diff in `apps/`.
- **Option B (rejected)**: add ~10 new hex literals approximating
  the Archive-room palette in sRGB. Would maintain aesthetic
  fidelity in fallback mode, but pushes the audit count from 24 to
  ~34 and demands a parallel WCAG measurement run on the new sRGB
  values.

**Decision: Option A.** Modern Chromium ships oklch (the e2e
target); the fallback is a **safety net for older builds and
non-Chromium browsers**, not a primary aesthetic surface. Document
the regression in the M2a progress-log entry so reviewers know it
exists. Hex isolation invariant: 24 holds exactly.

### 9.4 `--text-2xl` lands in M2a even though no consumer uses it yet

The empty-mark glyph (under §9.2) is the first consumer; future
consumers are the M5 chapter-break label and the eventual app title.
Landing the token in M2a centralizes the type-scale declaration even
when only one ratio is used now. The cost is 1 line in `tokens.css`;
the benefit is M5's chapter-break + the eventual app title both
read from a token instead of a literal.

### 9.5 JetBrains Mono prepended to existing mono stack

The Phase 4 baseline `--font-mono` is `ui-monospace, SFMono-Regular,
Menlo, Consolas, monospace`. M2a prepends `"JetBrains Mono"` per
spec line 38 + line 892. JetBrains Mono is **not** self-hosted —
the system fallback chain ensures every developer machine has a
high-quality monospace immediately even if the user has never
installed JetBrains Mono. If a future phase wants to self-host
JetBrains Mono, it adds another `@font-face` block in `tokens.css`
with the same `font-display: swap` recipe; the architecture
supports it without churn.

### 9.6 Subsetting recipe: `--layout-features=kern,liga,smcp,onum,ital`

The `smcp` (small caps) feature is required for the M5 chapter-break
label. The `onum` (old-style numerals) feature is required for the
editorial mood (timestamps look more "manuscript" with old-style
figures). The `ital` axis is required for the empty-pane preface +
the future skim chapter-break label. Dropping any of these is a
visible aesthetic regression. Keeping `kern` + `liga` is standard
hygiene for editorial type.

### 9.7 Designer's choice of size-adjust + ascent-override values is APPROXIMATE

The values in §4 (`size-adjust: 99.5%; ascent-override: 92%;
descent-override: 22%`) are starting values. The developer
**MUST** validate them against the actual subsetted woff2 and
adjust if needed. Acceptable visual outcome: zero layout shift > 1 px
when DevTools forces the swap. If the M2a measurement comes in with
shift > 1 px, the developer adjusts the override values and
re-measures; the design.md is updated with the final values in the
M2a progress-log entry.

### 9.8 The noise overlay rule could land in `SessionView.css` instead of `global.css`

The designer recommends `SessionView.css` because:
- The rule decorates one specific surface (`.session-pane`).
- `global.css` is meant for cross-cutting concerns (cascade, body,
  utility classes).
- Co-locating the rule with the surface means a future maintainer
  who deletes `SessionView.css` (unlikely but conceivable) cleans up
  the noise rule automatically.

The reduced-motion + dark-mode suppression rules co-locate with the
main rule. The developer picks; either works.

# M2a color notes

M2a is the **first chunk that adds tokens** since Phase 4. The
introductions enumerate below; the `var(--token)` references in M1a
+ M1b CSS keep working unchanged because of the Option B aliases.

## Tokens introduced or revised in M2a

| Token                    | Status   | Light                       | Dark                         |
|--------------------------|----------|-----------------------------|-------------------------------|
| `--color-surface`        | revised  | `oklch(98% 0.01 70)`        | `oklch(15% 0.01 70)`          |
| `--color-surface-raised` | revised  | `oklch(96% 0.01 70)`        | `oklch(18% 0.01 70)`          |
| `--color-ink`            | NEW      | `oklch(20% 0.02 70)`        | `oklch(92% 0.01 70)`          |
| `--color-ink-muted`      | NEW      | `oklch(45% 0.02 70)`        | `oklch(70% 0.01 70)`          |
| `--color-border`         | revised  | `oklch(85% 0.01 70)`        | `oklch(28% 0.01 70)`          |
| `--color-border-strong`  | revised  | `oklch(70% 0.02 70)`        | `oklch(40% 0.02 70)`          |
| `--color-accent`         | revised  | `oklch(60% 0.15 50)`        | `oklch(65% 0.15 50)`          |
| `--color-accent-hover`   | revised  | `oklch(50% 0.16 50)`        | `oklch(72% 0.13 50)`          |
| `--color-success`        | revised  | `oklch(48% 0.13 155)`       | `oklch(64% 0.13 155)`         |
| `--color-warn`           | revised  | `oklch(58% 0.15 60)`        | `oklch(72% 0.13 60)`          |
| `--color-error`          | revised  | `oklch(52% 0.18 25)`        | `oklch(68% 0.16 25)`          |

The four alias declarations (Option B):

| Alias                 | Resolves to             |
|-----------------------|--------------------------|
| `--color-text`        | `var(--color-ink)`       |
| `--color-text-muted`  | `var(--color-ink-muted)` |
| `--color-bg`          | `var(--color-surface)`   |
| `--font-sans`         | `var(--font-chrome)`     |

## Color-mix recipes (M1a + M1b carryover; retinted automatically)

These are unchanged from M1a/M1b — they consume `var(--color-accent)`
or `var(--color-ink)` and retint automatically when the underlying
token shifts to oklch. The developer does NOT re-author them.

| Recipe                                                                    | Used at                                          |
|---------------------------------------------------------------------------|--------------------------------------------------|
| `color-mix(in srgb, var(--color-ink) 4%, transparent)`                    | row hover tint (M1a; M1b previously read `--color-text` → aliased) |
| `color-mix(in srgb, var(--color-accent) 8%, transparent)`                 | selected row tint (resting)                      |
| `color-mix(in srgb, var(--color-accent) 12%, transparent)`                | selected + hover tint                            |
| `color-mix(in srgb, var(--color-accent) 22%, transparent)`                | deep-link pulse keyframe peak                    |
| `color-mix(in srgb, var(--color-success) 15%, var(--color-surface))`      | `.badge.up-to-date` background                   |
| `color-mix(in srgb, var(--color-success) 70%, var(--color-ink))`          | `.badge.up-to-date` foreground                   |
| `color-mix(in srgb, var(--color-success) 35%, var(--color-surface))`      | `.badge.up-to-date` border                       |
| `color-mix(in srgb, var(--color-{success|error|accent}) 65%, var(--color-border))` | toast variant border (3:1 SC 1.4.11 anchor)   |

The recipes use `in srgb` color space mixing. The new oklch source
tokens get converted to sRGB before the mix; the resulting tints are
slightly different from a hypothetical `in oklch` mix, but every
recipe was authored against `in srgb` in Phase 4 + M1a + M1b and
the WCAG measurements were calibrated against the sRGB output. **Do
not change `in srgb` to `in oklch`** in M2a — it would silently
shift every tinted surface and require a full WCAG re-run.

## Hex fallback layer

The 24 existing hex literals in `tokens.css` form the
`@supports not (color: oklch(0% 0 0))` fallback layer. The fallback
preserves the **Phase 4 cool-blue palette**, not the Archive-room
warm palette — see design.md §9.3 for the rationale. The hex audit
holds at exactly 24:

```bash
rg -n '#[0-9a-fA-F]{3,8}' apps/frontend/src/ | wc -l
# expected: 24 (all in tokens.css)
```

## Dark mode

All M2a color decisions have dark-mode oklch redefinitions in
`tokens.css`. The compositional behavior is automatic — none of the
11 feature-local CSS files needs a dark-mode override; everything
re-resolves through `var(...)` against the dark-mode token block.

## Status pill foreground/background ratios across the new ramp

The Phase 4 status pill recipe (`background = 15% mix; foreground =
70% mix; border = 35% mix`) was calibrated for the Phase 4 sRGB
palette. The §7 contrast table in design.md confirms the recipes
hold under the new oklch ramp (every pill clears AA at 4.5:1 on
both light + dark). The risky pair is the `.badge.outdated` on
`--color-surface` in light mode (4.7:1) — it sits closest to the
4.5:1 floor. If the developer's measurement comes in below 4.5:1
after oklch CIE2000 conversion, the mitigation is to bump the warn
foreground mix from 70% to 75%; this lifts contrast ~5% without
changing the visual character.

## `.title-cell-refresh` (the M1b decision)

M1b warmed `.title-cell-refresh` from `--color-text-muted` to
`--color-warn` (`#b86b07` light / `#e0a75a` dark in fallback;
`oklch(58% 0.15 60)` / `oklch(72% 0.13 60)` in oklch). The §7
contrast table confirms it still clears AA against both
`--color-surface` (4.9:1 light / 5.4:1 dark) and the selected-row
tint (4.6:1 light / 5.1:1 dark) under the new oklch ramp.

# M2a motion notes

M2a introduces the **first motion tokens**. The chunk's only behavioral
diff to motion (other than the token introduction) is the M1a deep-link
pulse keyframe migration from a literal `600ms ease-out` to
`var(--motion-pulse) var(--ease-out)`.

## New tokens

| Token                  | Value                              | Used by                                           |
|------------------------|-------------------------------------|---------------------------------------------------|
| `--motion-fast`        | `80ms`                             | row hover tint (M1a) — currently inline literal   |
| `--motion-base`        | `120ms`                            | selected row, M2b tab indicator, M4 page-turn fade|
| `--motion-disclosure`  | `200ms`                            | `<details>` `block-size` (M5 exemption)           |
| `--motion-pulse`       | `600ms`                            | deep-link pulse one-shot (M1a → M2a migrated)     |
| `--ease-standard`      | `cubic-bezier(0.4, 0, 0.2, 1)`     | M2b tab indicator slide                           |
| `--ease-out`           | `cubic-bezier(0.0, 0.0, 0.2, 1)`   | M1a pulse, M4 transcript fade                     |
| `--ease-in-out`        | `cubic-bezier(0.4, 0, 0.6, 1)`     | M5 disclosure block-size                          |

## Keyframe migration (the only motion diff in M2a)

In `apps/frontend/src/features/sessions/SessionsTable.css`:

**BEFORE M2a:**
```css
table tbody tr[data-deep-link="true"] {
  animation: deep-link-pulse 600ms ease-out 1;
}
```

**AFTER M2a:**
```css
table tbody tr[data-deep-link="true"] {
  animation: deep-link-pulse var(--motion-pulse) var(--ease-out) 1;
}
```

The `1` iteration count stays a literal — it is not a designed-in token,
it's the algorithmic count.

The `@keyframes deep-link-pulse` block itself does NOT change. Its `0%`
peak (`color-mix(... var(--color-accent) 22% ...)`) and `100%` resting
(`color-mix(... var(--color-accent) 8% ...)`) consume
`var(--color-accent)`, so the new oklch sienna retints the keyframe
automatically. **Critical**: the pulse on first paint after M2a
reads sienna instead of blue. Visual delta is large; aesthetic delta
is exactly the intent of the M2 chunk — the pulse becomes a signature
detail of Archive-room, not a generic system-blue UI flash.

## What M2a does NOT touch

- No new `@keyframes` blocks. The M5 chapter-break and M4 page-turn
  fade keyframes land in their own milestones.
- No M1a global reduced-motion zero-out edit — the existing rule in
  `global.css` (lines 145-154) already targets every M2a-related
  property (`animation-duration`, `transition-duration`,
  `scroll-behavior`). The only addition under reduced-motion is the
  `background-image: none` rule on `.session-pane` (the noise overlay
  is decorative; suppressed). That rule is documented in
  `design.md §9` and is **separate from** the motion zero-out.

## Reduced-motion behavior with the new tokens

With `prefers-reduced-motion: reduce` set, every transition collapses
to `0.01ms` per the global rule. The deep-link pulse, the row hover
tint, and the selected-row tint **all complete in one paint frame**.
The noise overlay disappears. The page is fully usable; no signal is
lost.

The motion tokens themselves (`--motion-pulse: 600ms`) do NOT change
under reduced motion — the global zero-out overrides them at the
property level via `transition-duration: 0.01ms !important`. The
tokens still hold their canonical values for any future code that
inspects them (e.g., a Playwright test asserting the token value).

## Performance budget posture

The M2a additions are token introductions, not new animated surfaces.
The performance budget commentary in §Motion / §Performance budget
of the spec applies to the M5 disclosure exemption, not to anything
M2a lands. Specifically:

- The deep-link pulse stays `background-color`-only (compositor-
  cheap; no layout).
- The reading wash is a static `background-image` (no animation; the
  decorative noise is rasterized once and cached by the browser).
- The motion tokens themselves are `:root`-scoped CSS variables
  (zero runtime cost; resolved at parse time).

No frame-time risk introduced by M2a.

## 12. Open questions for the reviewer

- **Noise-overlay opacity (3% vs 4%)**: spec line 28 says "~3%"; the
  SVG snippet at line 922 hardcodes `0.03`. The designer recommends
  staying at 3%, but if the reviewer feels the wash is invisible
  even on a calibrated display, bumping to 4% is a one-line edit
  inside the data URL (`feColorMatrix values='... 0 0 0 0.03 0' →
  '... 0 0 0 0.04 0'`). **Open for reviewer call.**
- **Empty-mark glyph `--text-2xl` vs `--text-xl`**: spec line 595
  is ambiguous about the glyph's size; the M1a design renders it at
  `--text-xl`. M2a designer recommends bumping to `--text-2xl` for
  the editorial moment, but if the reviewer prefers M1a continuity
  (the glyph as a discrete typographic mark, not a heading), the
  `--text-xl` version is cleaner. **Reviewer picks.**
- **Toast.info border 3.1:1 (light)**: this is the tightest WCAG-
  gated pair in the table and sits on the cusp. If the developer's
  measurement comes in below 3:1 (oklch interpolation drift), the
  designer recommends bumping the 65% mix to 70%. **Acceptable
  fallback, or does the reviewer want a different accent treatment
  for the info toast?**
- **`--text-base: 1rem` is 16px not the existing 0.95rem (~15.2px)**:
  the spec literal is `1rem`; this is a **+5% shift in body text
  size** that affects every Phase 4 surface that reads
  `var(--text-base)`. The designer's read: this is intentional; the
  Archive-room aesthetic wants generous editorial reading; a 1rem
  body is the editorial standard. The reviewer should confirm — if
  the body-size shift creates layout regressions the designer hasn't
  anticipated, the override is to keep the existing `0.95rem` and
  document the divergence. **Recommendation: ship the spec value
  (1rem); accept the +5% body-size shift.**

preface + empty-mark promotion. No tab primitive, no
`SessionView.tsx` rewire, no `Tabs.tsx` — those are M2b.

   246	/* ===== @font-face — Fraunces self-hosted (Phase 5 / M2a) =====
   247	 * Two declarations: Roman + Italic. The upstream Fraunces is shipped
   248	 * as two separate variable fonts (Roman, Italic) rather than one
   249	 * variable font with an italic axis, so each `font-style` value points
   250	 * at a different `.woff2` file. Both are subset to Latin Basic +
   251	 * Latin-1 Supplement + General Punctuation (`U+0020-007E,
   252	 * U+00A0-00FF, U+2010-2027, U+2030-2052`) with kerning, ligatures,
   253	 * small-caps, and old-style numerals. Subsetting + axis-pinning recipe
   254	 * documented at `docs/dependency-rules.md` §"Phase 5 M2a notes".
   255	 *
   256	 * `font-display: swap` — Fraunces NEVER blocks first paint. The
   257	 * fallback (Charter / Iowan Old Style / Georgia) renders immediately
   258	 * and Fraunces swaps in when the woff2 finishes loading. The
   259	 * `size-adjust` + `ascent-override` + `descent-override` values are
   260	 * tuned to Charter's metrics (the most common system serif on
   261	 * macOS / iOS) so the swap is invisible — line heights stay within
   262	 * 1 px on every step in the type scale. Designer-recommended starting
   263	 * values per design.md §"Fraunces fallback math"; revisit after the
   264	 * M6 manual font-swap measurement.
   265	 *
   266	 * Reversibility (Resolved Decision #15): deleting both @font-face
   267	 * blocks AND the two woff2 files yields a still-cohesive
   268	 * system-serif aesthetic. No font is required for the page to
   269	 * render. */
   270	@font-face {
   271	  font-family: "Fraunces";
   272	  font-style: normal;
   273	  font-weight: 100 900;
   274	  font-display: swap;
   275	  src: url("/fonts/Fraunces-subset.woff2") format("woff2-variations"),
   276	    url("/fonts/Fraunces-subset.woff2") format("woff2");
   277	  size-adjust: 99.5%;
   278	  ascent-override: 92%;
   279	  descent-override: 22%;
   280	  line-gap-override: 0%;
   281	  unicode-range: U+0020-007E, U+00A0-00FF, U+2010-2027, U+2030-2052;
   282	}

     2	 * Feature-local CSS for the Toast queue.
     3	 *
     4	 * Imported by `Toast.tsx` (sibling-import convention). Note that the
     5	 * `.toast-queue` selector lives here even though only `App.tsx` mounts
     6	 * the queue container; the queue + the per-toast styling are one
     7	 * cohesive surface, and colocating them keeps the M6 retirement of
     8	 * `app.css` clean (no third sheet just for the queue wrapper).
     9	 *
    10	 * Selectors owned here:
    11	 *   - `.toast-queue` — fixed-position stack at the bottom-right of
    12	 *     the viewport. Sibling-of-`<main>` placement is for fixed-position
    13	 *     LAYOUT only (NOT React render isolation): toast push/dismiss
    14	 *     re-renders the App subtree, but the merged/filtered/sorted/
    15	 *     paginated rows are `useMemo`d so the heavy computation does not
    16	 *     re-run on a toast event.
    17	 *   - `.toast` — the toast itself; sets surface + border + radius +
    18	 *     shadow + min-width.
    19	 *   - `.toast.success`, `.toast.error`, `.toast.info` — variant
    20	 *     border tints (color-mix to keep them readable against the
    21	 *     surface-raised background in both modes).
    22	 *   - `.toast-title` — bold leading line.
    23	 *   - `.toast-message` — muted secondary line.
    24	 *   - `.toast-details`, `.toast-details summary` — collapsible
    25	 *     `<details>` disclosure for the structured RescanReport /
    26	 *     ImportReport counts (collapsed by default).
    27	 *   - `.toast-actions`, `.toast-actions button`,
    28	 *     `.toast-actions button:hover` — the Retry + Dismiss buttons.
    29	 *
    30	 * Structural literals (per design-balloon mitigation):
    31	 *   - `max-width: 26rem` on `.toast-queue` — keeps the fixed-position
    32	 *     stack from blowing across the viewport.
    33	 *   - `min-width: 18rem` on `.toast` — so a single short toast still
    34	 *     reads as a deliberate notification rather than a tiny chip.
    35	 *   - `z-index: 2` on `.toast-queue` — layering literal (the design
    36	 *     system has no token for stacking layers; promoting this would
    37	 *     be speculative for two consumers — `.action-bar.sticky` at
    38	 *     `z-index: 1`, `.toast-queue` at `z-index: 2`). The drawer's
    39	 *     `top-layer` (native `<dialog>`) sits above both regardless.
    40	 */
    41	
    42	/* Toast queue — fixed-position stack at the bottom-right corner of
    43	 * the viewport. Sibling-of-`<main>` placement is for fixed-position
    44	 * layout only (NOT React render isolation): toast push/dismiss
    45	 * re-renders the App subtree, but the merged/filtered/sorted/
    46	 * paginated rows are `useMemo`d so the heavy computation does not
    47	 * re-run on a toast event. */

    59	
    60	/* Each toast inside the queue. `pointer-events: auto` re-enables

    72	}
    73	
    74	/* Toast variant identity is conveyed by border color alone (no per-variant
    75	 * background fill), so the border MUST clear WCAG SC 1.4.11 non-text
    76	 * contrast (3:1 against `--color-surface-raised`). The original 50% mix
    77	 * against `--color-border` failed in light (2.5-2.8 range). Phase 4 M6
    78	 * lifted to 65% (verified 3.07-3.83 in both modes via the M6 contrast
    79	 * script — see WCAG table in `progress/phase-4.progress.md`).
    80	 *
    81	 * Phase 5 / M2a bumps `.toast.info` to 70% as a pre-emptive mitigation
    82	 * per UI/UX reviewer Q3 + design.md §7. Reasoning: M2a swaps the
    83	 * `--color-accent` from the Phase 4 cool-blue literal to oklch sienna
    84	 * (`oklch(60% 0.15 50)`); the Phase 4 measurement of 3.07:1 was
    85	 * borderline against the cool-blue accent, and oklch `color-mix`
    86	 * interpolation can drift slightly from sRGB-anchored expectations.
    87	 * Bumping the info variant to 70% restores the safety margin (designer
    88	 * estimate ~3.3:1 light, ~3.8:1 dark) without touching the success /
    89	 * error variants, which sit on different-hue color tokens unaffected
    90	 * by the accent palette swap. Codex precedent at Phase 4 M6 caught a
    91	 * sub-3:1 toast border the designer had reported as passing — this
    92	 * preemptive bump is the same risk surface mitigated proactively.
    93	 * The 70% literal here will be re-verified with the M6 contrast script
    94	 * once the WCAG sweep runs against the implementation tokens. Badges
    95	 * use a different recipe (35%) because their identity is the filled
    96	 * tinted background + AA-passing pill text, so the badge border is
    97	 * decorative reinforcement under SC 1.4.11. */
    98	.toast.success {
    99	  border-color: color-mix(in srgb, var(--color-success) 65%, var(--color-border));
   100	}

   103	  border-color: color-mix(in srgb, var(--color-error) 65%, var(--color-border));
   104	}

   108	}

     1	import { StrictMode } from "react";
     2	import { createRoot } from "react-dom/client";
     3	
     4	// CSS load order is load-bearing. The three global sheets are imported
     5	// FIRST so Vite emits them ahead of the feature-local sibling sheets
     6	// pulled in transitively via `App`'s import graph. `reset.css` nukes
     7	// browser defaults; `tokens.css` defines the CSS custom properties the
     8	// rest reference (including under `prefers-color-scheme: dark`);
     9	// `global.css` applies token-driven body / `<main>` shell rules and
    10	// the four global utility classes (`.muted`, `.mono`, `.stack`,
    11	// `.empty`). Feature-local CSS is imported by sibling `.tsx`/`.ts`
    12	// files (e.g. `SessionsTable.tsx` imports `./SessionsTable.css`); they
    13	// land after the globals in the dist bundle so feature rules can
    14	// override globals where needed. The Phase 4 Milestone 6 retirement
    15	// of `app.css` collapsed the prior monolith into per-feature siblings;
    16	// see `progress/phase-4.progress.md` Chunk G for the migration map.
    17	import "./styles/reset.css";
    18	import "./styles/tokens.css";
    19	import "./styles/global.css";
    20	
    21	import { App } from "./App";
    22	
    23	const container = document.getElementById("root");
    24	if (!container) {
    25	  throw new Error("Root container #root not found in index.html");
    26	}
    27	
    28	createRoot(container).render(
    29	  <StrictMode>
    30	    <App />
    31	  </StrictMode>,
    32	);

     1	/*

     4	 * Loaded after `reset.css` and `tokens.css` so the cascade resolves:
     5	 * browser defaults → reset → tokens → global → feature-local imports
     6	 * (sibling `.css` files imported by their matching `.tsx`/`.ts`,
     7	 * concatenated by Vite into the dist bundle).

    10	 *   - `body` — token-driven background, foreground, font-family,
    11	 *     padding shell.
    12	 *   - `code, kbd, samp, pre` — monospace font for inline code rendering.

    14	 *     900 px: `grid-template-columns: minmax(300px, 380px) 1fr`
    15	 *     (compact list left + reading content right). Below 900 px:
    16	 *     single column; the `data-narrow-mode` attribute on `<main>`
    17	 *     drives which pane is visible (the other stays React-mounted
    18	 *     so scroll/tab/disclosure state survives the toggle).

    20	 *     narrow viewport @media query; the wide-viewport hairline gutter
    21	 *     is `border-right` on `.list-pane`.
    22	 *   - `h1` — zeroed margin so the page title sits flush with the
    23	 *     surrounding stack.
    24	 *   - `.muted`, `.mono`, `.stack`, `.empty` — global text/structure
    25	 *     utilities reused across feature surfaces (table cells, drawer
    26	 *     metadata, empty-state callouts, action bar captions). These four
    27	 *     are intentionally global because they have no single owning
    28	 *     feature; they read like text-level mixins.
    29	 *
    30	 * Phase 5 / M1a structural literals introduced here (matched against
    31	 * `working/phase-5.md` §Structural literals lines 926–946):
    32	 *   - `grid-template-columns: minmax(300px, 380px) 1fr` on `<main>` (M1)

    39	 * `margin: var(--space-6) auto var(--space-8)`, `gap: var(--space-6)`)
    40	 * is REPLACED by the Phase-5 recipe per spec line 497. Only
    41	 * `body { padding: var(--space-6) }` carries over verbatim.
    42	 *
    43	 * Phase 4 Milestone 6 history: when `app.css` was retired, the body
    44	 * shell and the four utility classes (`.muted`, `.mono`, `.stack`,
    45	 * `.empty`) moved here. Component- and feature-specific rules moved
    46	 * to sibling `.css` files imported by the matching `.tsx`/`.ts`. See
    47	 * `progress/phase-4.progress.md` Chunk G for the full migration map.
    48	 */

    89	 * single 1 px line drawn as `border-right` on `.list-pane`. No grid-
    90	 * gap, no styled rail, no shadow — the hairline IS the only visual
    91	 * separation between panes. It only renders on wide viewports because
    92	 * the narrow-mode CSS (below) hides one of the panes; with only one
    93	 * pane visible there is nothing to gutter against.
    94	 */
    95	.list-pane {
    96	  min-inline-size: 0;
    97	  display: flex;
    98	  flex-direction: column;
    99	}
   100	
   101	@media (min-width: 900px) {
   102	  .list-pane {
   103	    border-right: 1px solid var(--color-border);
   104	  }
   105	}
   106	
   107	/*
   108	 * Narrow-viewport visibility. Both panes stay React-mounted so the
   109	 * SessionView's tab state + the list panel's scroll position survive
   110	 * the toggle (per spec line 599). The `data-narrow-mode` attribute on
   111	 * `<main>` drives which pane is visible; the other carries
   112	 * `display: none`.
   113	 */
   114	@media (max-width: 899.98px) {
   115	  main.split-pane[data-narrow-mode="session"] .list-pane {
   116	    display: none;
   117	  }
   118	  main.split-pane[data-narrow-mode="list"] .session-pane {
   119	    display: none;
   120	  }
   121	  /*
   122	   * "← Back to list" affordance — re-enables the quiet text-link button
   123	   * that `SessionView.css` declares with `display: none` by default.
   124	   * Visible only on narrow viewports AND when narrowMode === "session"
   125	   * (matches design.md §3.5 + spec acceptance item #24). This rule is
   126	   * co-located with the rest of the data-narrow-mode visibility rules
   127	   * (rather than in `SessionView.css`) so the pattern stays in one
   128	   * place and a future maintainer can audit the full narrow-mode
   129	   * surface in a single file.
   130	   */
   131	  main.split-pane[data-narrow-mode="session"] .back-to-list {
   132	    display: inline-block;
   133	  }
   134	}
   135	
   136	/*
   137	 * Reduced-motion zero-out. Spec lines 102–110 verbatim. This is the
   138	 * single global rule that suppresses every Phase-5 animation under
   139	 * `prefers-reduced-motion: reduce`: the M1a deep-link pulse, the M2
   140	 * tab indicator slide, the M4 transcript fade, the M5 skim-block
   141	 * stagger — all of them collapse to one paint frame. The signature
   142	 * noise-overlay (M2) is also decorative and disappears under this
   143	 * query.
   144	 */
   145	@media (prefers-reduced-motion: reduce) {
   146	  *,
   147	  *::before,
   148	  *::after {
   149	    animation-duration: 0.01ms !important;
   150	    animation-iteration-count: 1 !important;
   151	    transition-duration: 0.01ms !important;
   152	    scroll-behavior: auto !important;
   153	  }
   154	}

   157	  margin: 0;
   158	}

   162	}

+/* ===== @supports hex fallback layer (Phase 5 / M2a NEW) =====
+ * For browsers without native `oklch()` support (Chromium <111,
+ * Firefox <113, Safari <15.4) the canonical color tokens fall back to
+ * the Phase 4 hex literals. The aesthetic regression (Phase 4
+ * cool-blue palette renders instead of Archive-room warm-paper) is
+ * documented + accepted per design.md §9.3 — the fallback is a
+ * cohesive Phase 4 design, only the hue differs.
+ *
+ * Hex isolation invariant: every `#`-prefixed literal in
+ * `apps/frontend/src/` lives inside this block. Total = 24
+ * (12 light + 12 dark). The aliases (`--color-text` etc.) and the
+ * canonical declarations above continue to work — `var(...)`
+ * references resolve through the aliases to the canonical name, and
+ * the canonical name picks up either the oklch value (modern browsers)
+ * or the hex (legacy browsers). */
+@supports not (color: oklch(0% 0 0)) {
+  :root {
+    --color-bg: #ffffff;
+    --color-surface: #f8f9fb;
+    --color-surface-raised: #ffffff;
+    --color-border: #e3e5ea;
+    --color-border-strong: #c8ccd4;
+    --color-ink: #14161a;
+    --color-ink-muted: #5a606b;
+    --color-accent: #2864d4;
+    --color-accent-hover: #1d4fa8;
+    --color-success: #1f7a4a;
+    --color-warn: #b86b07;
+    --color-error: #b13838;
+  }
+
+  @media (prefers-color-scheme: dark) {
+    :root {
+      --color-bg: #0f1115;
+      --color-surface: #151821;
+      --color-surface-raised: #1b1f2a;
+      --color-border: #262b36;
+      --color-border-strong: #3a404d;
+      --color-ink: #e8eaef;
+      --color-ink-muted: #9098a6;
+      --color-accent: #6da5ff;
+      --color-accent-hover: #8bbbff;
+      --color-success: #5fb68a;
+      --color-warn: #e0a75a;
+      --color-error: #e57d7d;
+    }
+  }
+}

+/* ===== @font-face — Fraunces self-hosted (Phase 5 / M2a) =====
+ * Two declarations: Roman + Italic. The upstream Fraunces is shipped
+ * as two separate variable fonts (Roman, Italic) rather than one
+ * variable font with an italic axis, so each `font-style` value points
+ * at a different `.woff2` file. Both are subset to Latin Basic +
+ * Latin-1 Supplement + General Punctuation (`U+0020-007E,
+ * U+00A0-00FF, U+2010-2027, U+2030-2052`) with kerning, ligatures,
+ * small-caps, and old-style numerals. Subsetting + axis-pinning recipe
+ * documented at `docs/dependency-rules.md` §"Phase 5 M2a notes".
+ *
+ * `font-display: swap` — Fraunces NEVER blocks first paint. The
+ * fallback (Charter / Iowan Old Style / Georgia) renders immediately
+ * and Fraunces swaps in when the woff2 finishes loading. The
+ * `size-adjust` + `ascent-override` + `descent-override` values are
+ * tuned to Charter's metrics (the most common system serif on
+ * macOS / iOS) so the swap is invisible — line heights stay within
+ * 1 px on every step in the type scale. Designer-recommended starting
+ * values per design.md §"Fraunces fallback math"; revisit after the
+ * M6 manual font-swap measurement.
+ *
+ * Reversibility (Resolved Decision #15): deleting both @font-face
+ * blocks AND the two woff2 files yields a still-cohesive
+ * system-serif aesthetic. No font is required for the page to
+ * render. */
+@font-face {
+  font-family: "Fraunces";
+  font-style: normal;
+  font-weight: 100 900;
+  font-display: swap;
+  src: url("/fonts/Fraunces-subset.woff2") format("woff2-variations"),
+    url("/fonts/Fraunces-subset.woff2") format("woff2");
+  size-adjust: 99.5%;
+  ascent-override: 92%;
+  descent-override: 22%;
+  line-gap-override: 0%;
+  unicode-range: U+0020-007E, U+00A0-00FF, U+2010-2027, U+2030-2052;
+}
+
+@font-face {
+  font-family: "Fraunces";
+  font-style: italic;
+  font-weight: 100 900;
+  font-display: swap;
+  src: url("/fonts/Fraunces-Italic-subset.woff2") format("woff2-variations"),
+    url("/fonts/Fraunces-Italic-subset.woff2") format("woff2");
+  size-adjust: 99.5%;
+  ascent-override: 92%;
+  descent-override: 22%;
+  line-gap-override: 0%;
+  unicode-range: U+0020-007E, U+00A0-00FF, U+2010-2027, U+2030-2052;

The token and CSS structure mostly lines up so far. I’m doing an independent contrast calculation from the actual `oklch()` and `color-mix(in srgb, ...)` values now, because the M2a artifact explicitly warns that the tight toast and border pairs should not be accepted from the design table alone.

/bin/bash -lc "python3 - <<'PY'
import math

def oklch(L_percent, C, h_deg):
    L=L_percent/100
    h=math.radians(h_deg)
    a=C*math.cos(h); b=C*math.sin(h)
    l_=L+0.3963377774*a+0.2158037573*b
    m_=L-0.1055613458*a-0.0638541728*b
    s_=L-0.0894841775*a-1.2914855480*b
    l=l_**3; m=m_**3; s=s_**3
    r=+4.0767416621*l-3.3077115913*m+0.2309699292*s
    g=-1.2684380046*l+2.6097574011*m-0.3413193965*s
    b=-0.0041960863*l-0.7034186147*m+1.7076147010*s
    return tuple(max(0,min(1,x)) for x in (r,g,b))

def srgb_to_linear(c):
    return c/12.92 if c <= 0.04045 else ((c+0.055)/1.055)**2.4

def rel_lum(rgb):
    r,g,b=[srgb_to_linear(c) for c in rgb]
    return 0.2126*r+0.7152*g+0.0722*b

def contrast(a,b):
    la,lb=rel_lum(a),rel_lum(b)
    hi,lo=max(la,lb),min(la,lb)
    return (hi+0.05)/(lo+0.05)

def mix(c1,p,c2):
    # CSS color-mix(in srgb, c1 p%, c2) with opaque colors, gamma-encoded sRGB coords.
    return tuple(c1[i]*p+c2[i]*(1-p) for i in range(3))

def over(fg, alpha, bg):
    return tuple(fg[i]*alpha + bg[i]*(1-alpha) for i in range(3))

def hx(s):
    s=s.lstrip('#')
    return tuple(int(s[i:i+2],16)/255 for i in (0,2,4))

light={
 'surface':oklch(98,.01,70),'raised':oklch(96,.01,70),'ink':oklch(20,.02,70),'muted':oklch(45,.02,70),
 'border':oklch(85,.01,70),'border_strong':oklch(70,.02,70),'accent':oklch(60,.15,50),'success':oklch(48,.13,155),'warn':oklch(58,.15,60),'error':oklch(52,.18,25)
}
dark={
 'surface':oklch(15,.01,70),'raised':oklch(18,.01,70),'ink':oklch(92,.01,70),'muted':oklch(70,.01,70),
 'border':oklch(28,.01,70),'border_strong':oklch(40,.02,70),'accent':oklch(65,.15,50),'success':oklch(64,.13,155),'warn':oklch(72,.13,60),'error':oklch(68,.16,25)
}
hex_light={
 'bg':hx('#ffffff'),'surface':hx('#f8f9fb'),'raised':hx('#ffffff'),'ink':hx('#14161a'),'muted':hx('#5a606b'),
 'border':hx('#e3e5ea'),'border_strong':hx('#c8ccd4'),'accent':hx('#2864d4'),'success':hx('#1f7a4a'),'warn':hx('#b86b07'),'error':hx('#b13838')
}
hex_dark={
 'bg':hx('#0f1115'),'surface':hx('#151821'),'raised':hx('#1b1f2a'),'ink':hx('#e8eaef'),'muted':hx('#9098a6'),
 'border':hx('#262b36'),'border_strong':hx('#3a404d'),'accent':hx('#6da5ff'),'success':hx('#5fb68a'),'warn':hx('#e0a75a'),'error':hx('#e57d7d')
}

def row(label, value):
    print(f'{label:52s} {value:.2f}:1')

for name,t in [('oklch light',light),('oklch dark',dark),('hex light',hex_light),('hex dark',hex_dark)]:
    print('\\n'+name)
    row('ink/surface',contrast(t['ink'],t['surface']))
    row('muted/surface',contrast(t['muted'],t['surface']))
    row('ink/raised',contrast(t['ink'],t['raised']))
    row('muted/raised',contrast(t['muted'],t['raised']))
    row('border/surface',contrast(t['border'],t['surface']))
    row('border_strong/surface',contrast(t['border_strong'],t['surface']))
    row('accent/surface',contrast(t['accent'],t['surface']))
    for sem in ['success','accent','warn','error']:
        fg=mix(t[sem],.70,t['ink'])
        bg=mix(t[sem],.15,t['surface'])
        row(f'badge {sem} fg/bg',contrast(fg,bg))
    for sem,p in [('success',.65),('error',.65),('accent/info70',.70)]:
        key='accent' if sem.startswith('accent') else sem
        border=mix(t[key],p,t['border'])
        row(f'toast {sem} border/raised',contrast(border,t['raised']))
    # Row tints approximate semi-transparent color over surface; color-mix with transparent p renders p alpha color.
    row('ink on hover 4% ink over surface',contrast(t['ink'],over(t['ink'],.04,t['surface'])))
    row('ink on selected 8% accent over surface',contrast(t['ink'],over(t['accent'],.08,t['surface'])))
    row('ink on pulse 22% accent over surface',contrast(t['ink'],over(t['accent'],.22,t['surface'])))

import math

def lin_to_srgb(c):
    c=max(0,min(1,c))
    return 12.92*c if c <= 0.0031308 else 1.055*(c**(1/2.4))-0.055

def srgb_to_linear(c):
    return c/12.92 if c <= 0.04045 else ((c+0.055)/1.055)**2.4

def oklch(L_percent, C, h_deg):
    L=L_percent/100
    h=math.radians(h_deg)
    a=C*math.cos(h); b=C*math.sin(h)
    l_=L+0.3963377774*a+0.2158037573*b
    m_=L-0.1055613458*a-0.0638541728*b
    s_=L-0.0894841775*a-1.2914855480*b
    l=l_**3; m=m_**3; s=s_**3
    r=+4.0767416621*l-3.3077115913*m+0.2309699292*s
    g=-1.2684380046*l+2.6097574011*m-0.3413193965*s
    b=-0.0041960863*l-0.7034186147*m+1.7076147010*s
    return tuple(lin_to_srgb(x) for x in (r,g,b))

def rel_lum(rgb):
    r,g,b=[srgb_to_linear(c) for c in rgb]
    return 0.2126*r+0.7152*g+0.0722*b

def contrast(a,b):
    la,lb=rel_lum(a),rel_lum(b)
    hi,lo=max(la,lb),min(la,lb)
    return (hi+0.05)/(lo+0.05)

def mix(c1,p,c2):
    return tuple(c1[i]*p+c2[i]*(1-p) for i in range(3))

def over(fg, alpha, bg):
    return tuple(fg[i]*alpha + bg[i]*(1-alpha) for i in range(3))

def hx(s):
    s=s.lstrip('#')
    return tuple(int(s[i:i+2],16)/255 for i in (0,2,4))

 'surface':oklch(98,.01,70),'raised':oklch(96,.01,70),'ink':oklch(20,.02,70),'muted':oklch(45,.02,70),

}

/bin/bash -lc "python3 - <<'PY'
# quick exact accent text contrasts from current tokens
import math

def lin_to_srgb(c):
    c=max(0,min(1,c)); return 12.92*c if c<=0.0031308 else 1.055*(c**(1/2.4))-0.055

def srgb_to_linear(c): return c/12.92 if c<=0.04045 else ((c+0.055)/1.055)**2.4

def oklch(L,C,h):
    L/=100; h=math.radians(h); a=C*math.cos(h); b=C*math.sin(h)
    l_=L+0.3963377774*a+0.2158037573*b; m_=L-0.1055613458*a-0.0638541728*b; s_=L-0.0894841775*a-1.2914855480*b
    l=l_**3; m=m_**3; s=s_**3
    return tuple(lin_to_srgb(x) for x in (4.0767416621*l-3.3077115913*m+0.2309699292*s, -1.2684380046*l+2.6097574011*m-0.3413193965*s, -0.0041960863*l-0.7034186147*m+1.7076147010*s))

def lum(rgb):
    r,g,b=[srgb_to_linear(c) for c in rgb]; return .2126*r+.7152*g+.0722*b

def cr(a,b):
    x,y=lum(a),lum(b); return (max(x,y)+.05)/(min(x,y)+.05)
for mode, vals in {
 'light': {'surface':oklch(98,.01,70),'raised':oklch(96,.01,70),'accent':oklch(60,.15,50),'hover':oklch(50,.16,50)},
 'dark': {'surface':oklch(15,.01,70),'raised':oklch(18,.01,70),'accent':oklch(65,.15,50),'hover':oklch(72,.13,50)},
}.items():
    print(mode)
    for fg in ['accent','hover']:
        for bg in ['surface','raised']:
            print(f'{fg}/{bg}: {cr(vals[fg],vals[bg]):.2f}:1')

import math

def lin_to_srgb(c):
    c=max(0,min(1,c)); return 12.92*c if c<=0.0031308 else 1.055*(c**(1/2.4))-0.055

def srgb_to_linear(c): return c/12.92 if c<=0.04045 else ((c+0.055)/1.055)**2.4

def oklch(L,C,h):
    L/=100; h=math.radians(h); a=C*math.cos(h); b=C*math.sin(h)
    l_=L+0.3963377774*a+0.2158037573*b; m_=L-0.1055613458*a-0.0638541728*b; s_=L-0.0894841775*a-1.2914855480*b
    l=l_**3; m=m_**3; s=s_**3
    return tuple(lin_to_srgb(x) for x in (4.0767416621*l-3.3077115913*m+0.2309699292*s, -1.2684380046*l+2.6097574011*m-0.3413193965*s, -0.0041960863*l-0.7034186147*m+1.7076147010*s))

def lum(rgb):
    r,g,b=[srgb_to_linear(c) for c in rgb]; return .2126*r+.7152*g+.0722*b

def cr(a,b):
    x,y=lum(a),lum(b); return (max(x,y)+.05)/(min(x,y)+.05)

def hx(rgb): return '#' + ''.join(f'{round(c*255):02x}' for c in rgb)
for surfaceL, mode in [(98,'light'),(15,'dark')]:
    surface=oklch(surfaceL,.01,70)
    print(mode, 'surface', hx(surface))
    for L in range(30,81):
        val=oklch(L,.02,70)
        ratio=cr(val,surface)
        if (mode=='light' and ratio>=3 and L < surfaceL) or (mode=='dark' and ratio>=3 and L > surfaceL):
            print('first integer L', L, hx(val), f'{ratio:.2f}')
            break
    for L in [65,64,63,62,61,60,48,49,50,51,52]:
        ratio=cr(oklch(L,.02,70),surface)
        if 2.8 <= ratio <= 3.5:
            print(L, hx(oklch(L,.02,70)), f'{ratio:.2f}')

/bin/bash -lc "python3 - <<'PY'
import math

def lin_to_srgb(c):
    c=max(0,min(1,c)); return 12.92*c if c<=0.0031308 else 1.055*(c**(1/2.4))-0.055

def srgb_to_linear(c): return c/12.92 if c<=0.04045 else ((c+0.055)/1.055)**2.4

def oklch(L,C,h):
    L/=100; h=math.radians(h); a=C*math.cos(h); b=C*math.sin(h)
    l_=L+0.3963377774*a+0.2158037573*b; m_=L-0.1055613458*a-0.0638541728*b; s_=L-0.0894841775*a-1.2914855480*b
    l=l_**3; m=m_**3; s=s_**3
    return tuple(lin_to_srgb(x) for x in (4.0767416621*l-3.3077115913*m+0.2309699292*s, -1.2684380046*l+2.6097574011*m-0.3413193965*s, -0.0041960863*l-0.7034186147*m+1.7076147010*s))

def lum(rgb):
    r,g,b=[srgb_to_linear(c) for c in rgb]; return .2126*r+.7152*g+.0722*b

def cr(a,b):
    x,y=lum(a),lum(b); return (max(x,y)+.05)/(min(x,y)+.05)

def hx(rgb): return '#' + ''.join(f'{round(c*255):02x}' for c in rgb)
surface=oklch(98,.01,70); raised=oklch(96,.01,70); ink=oklch(20,.02,70)

     1	# Dependency Rules
     2	
     3	## Allowed Directions
     4	
     5	- `apps/backend` may depend on:
     6	  - `components/collector-runtime`
     7	  - `components/configuration`
     8	  - `components/ingest-service`
     9	  - `components/observability`
    10	  - `components/raw-session-store`
    11	  - `components/ui-api-contracts`
    12	- `apps/frontend` (Bun + Vite + React + TypeScript) may depend on:
    13	  - `components/ui-api-contracts/bindings/*.ts` (generated TypeScript types; one-way import only)
    14	  - external npm packages managed via Bun for UI, HTTP fetch, and test tooling. Phase 4 adopted a strict 2-package escape-hatch budget for browser-only concerns native APIs cannot meet; one slot is consumed (`focus-trap-react@^11`, fixing a native `<dialog>` Tab-cycling gap), one slot remains reserved (`@tanstack/react-virtual`, gated on documented evidence that pagination plus `useMemo` cannot keep render time bounded). Adding a third escape-hatch package requires a fresh planning round.
    15	  - all backend communication is over HTTP to `apps/backend`
    16	- `components/ingest-service` may depend on:
    17	  - `components/collector-runtime`
    18	  - `components/raw-session-store`
    19	- `components/raw-session-store` may depend on:
    20	  - `components/ui-api-contracts`
    21	- `components/collector-runtime` may depend on:
    22	  - `components/ui-api-contracts`
    23	- `components/configuration`, `components/observability`, and `components/ui-api-contracts` should not depend on app crates
    24	
    25	## Forbidden Directions
    26	
    27	- `apps/frontend` must not depend on:
    28	  - `components/collector-runtime`
    29	  - `components/ingest-service`
    30	  - `components/raw-session-store`
    31	- No component crate may depend on `apps/backend` or `apps/frontend`
    32	- `apps/backend` must not render end-user HTML
    33	- `apps/frontend` must not reach into storage or ingest internals directly
    34	
    35	## Contract Handling
    36	
    37	- Shared page and JSON payloads live in `components/ui-api-contracts`
    38	- `components/ui-api-contracts/src/lib.rs` is the single source of truth for the contract. The TypeScript declarations under `components/ui-api-contracts/bindings/` are checked-in generated artifacts derived from `src/lib.rs` by the `ts-bindings` cargo feature — they are the canonical downstream output and must stay in sync with the Rust source: regenerate and commit them together with any Rust contract change, never hand-edited
    39	- The Bun frontend consumes contract types via one-way TypeScript imports from `components/ui-api-contracts/bindings/*.ts`, using the `@contracts/*` path alias wired in `apps/frontend/tsconfig.json` (or a direct relative path). The frontend MUST NOT re-declare contract types by hand; the thin barrel at `apps/frontend/src/lib/contracts.ts` re-exports the generated types for internal consumption
    40	- If a backend JSON shape changes, update:
    41	  - `components/ui-api-contracts/src/lib.rs`
    42	  - `components/ui-api-contracts/bindings/*.ts` (regenerate via `cargo test -p distill-portal-ui-api-contracts --features ts-bindings -- --ignored regenerate_ts_bindings`; see `docs/dev-commands.md`)
    43	  - `apps/backend/src/http_api.rs`
    44	  - the typed API layer in `apps/frontend/src/lib/` (`api.ts`, `contracts.ts`)
    45	  - any impacted frontend rendering under `apps/frontend/src/App.tsx`, the unified session feature at `apps/frontend/src/features/sessions/`, or the shared React primitives under `apps/frontend/src/components/`
    46	  - the relevant tests in `apps/backend/tests/http_api.rs`, `apps/frontend/src/App.test.tsx`, `apps/frontend/e2e/inspection.spec.ts`, and `tests/e2e/tests/inspection_surface.rs`
    47	
    48	## Layer Ownership
    49	
    50	- Storage writes and reads belong to `components/raw-session-store`
    51	- Replace-on-sync ingest rules belong to `components/ingest-service`
    52	- Source discovery and parsing belong to `components/collector-runtime`
    53	- Environment variable mapping belongs to `components/configuration`
    54	- HTTP routes belong to the app crates only
    55	
    56	## Frontend Dev-Time Topology
    57	
    58	- `apps/frontend/` is a Bun-managed package (`package.json`, `bun.lock`, `vite.config.ts`, `index.html`, `src/*.tsx`).
    59	- Frontend dev-time proxying of `/api/v1/**` and `/health` to the backend belongs in `apps/frontend/vite.config.ts`, not in any application code path.
    60	- The Bun app must continue to honor the frontend-boundary rules above: no direct dependency on `components/collector-runtime`, `components/ingest-service`, or `components/raw-session-store`; all backend communication goes over HTTP to the Rust backend.
    61	
    62	### Phase 5 M2a notes — Fraunces self-hosted as static asset
    63	
    64	Phase 5 introduces a single self-hosted variable serif (Fraunces) for the Archive-room display layer (empty-pane preface, future M5 chapter-break labels, future app title). The font ships as two `.woff2` files at `apps/frontend/public/fonts/Fraunces-subset.woff2` (Roman) and `apps/frontend/public/fonts/Fraunces-Italic-subset.woff2` (Italic), referenced by two `@font-face` blocks in `apps/frontend/src/styles/tokens.css`. Per Phase 5 §Resolved Decision #15, **a self-hosted font file is a static asset, not a runtime dependency** — the 2-package escape-hatch budget (currently 1/2 consumed by `focus-trap-react@^11`) is **not affected**. No bundler plugin, font-loading library, or JS dep is involved; Vite serves the woff2 files directly from `public/`. To regenerate (only when upstream Fraunces revs, ~yearly per Google Fonts cadence): download both variable fonts from `https://github.com/undercasetype/Fraunces` (`fonts/variable/Fraunces[SOFT,WONK,opsz,wght].ttf` + the Italic counterpart), run `fonttools varLib.instancer <input.ttf> SOFT=50 WONK=0 -o <pinned.ttf>` to drop the design-time-only SOFT and WONK axes (saves ~70 KB per file), then `pyftsubset <pinned.ttf> --output-file=apps/frontend/public/fonts/Fraunces-subset.woff2 --unicodes='U+0020-007E,U+00A0-00FF,U+2010-2027,U+2030-2052' --layout-features='kern,liga,smcp,onum' --flavor=woff2 --desubroutinize`. Target file size: ~80 KB per file. Reversibility: deleting both `@font-face` blocks AND both woff2 files yields a still-cohesive system-serif aesthetic (Charter / Iowan Old Style / Georgia at ~99% of Fraunces's visual size via the `size-adjust` override, no broken layout).

+Phase 5 introduces a single self-hosted variable serif (Fraunces) for the Archive-room display layer (empty-pane preface, future M5 chapter-break labels, future app title). The font ships as two `.woff2` files at `apps/frontend/public/fonts/Fraunces-subset.woff2` (Roman) and `apps/frontend/public/fonts/Fraunces-Italic-subset.woff2` (Italic), referenced by two `@font-face` blocks in `apps/frontend/src/styles/tokens.css`. Per Phase 5 §Resolved Decision #15, **a self-hosted font file is a static asset, not a runtime dependency** — the 2-package escape-hatch budget (currently 1/2 consumed by `focus-trap-react@^11`) is **not affected**. No bundler plugin, font-loading library, or JS dep is involved; Vite serves the woff2 files directly from `public/`. To regenerate (only when upstream Fraunces revs, ~yearly per Google Fonts cadence): download both variable fonts from `https://github.com/undercasetype/Fraunces` (`fonts/variable/Fraunces[SOFT,WONK,opsz,wght].ttf` + the Italic counterpart), run `fonttools varLib.instancer <input.ttf> SOFT=50 WONK=0 -o <pinned.ttf>` to drop the design-time-only SOFT and WONK axes (saves ~70 KB per file), then `pyftsubset <pinned.ttf> --output-file=apps/frontend/public/fonts/Fraunces-subset.woff2 --unicodes='U+0020-007E,U+00A0-00FF,U+2010-2027,U+2030-2052' --layout-features='kern,liga,smcp,onum' --flavor=woff2 --desubroutinize`. Target file size: ~80 KB per file. Reversibility: deleting both `@font-face` blocks AND both woff2 files yields a still-cohesive system-serif aesthetic (Charter / Iowan Old Style / Georgia at ~99% of Fraunces's visual size via the `size-adjust` override, no broken layout).

# compute toast info border contrast if accent light L changed, with current border/raised
import math

def lin_to_srgb(c):
    c=max(0,min(1,c)); return 12.92*c if c<=0.0031308 else 1.055*(c**(1/2.4))-0.055

def srgb_to_linear(c): return c/12.92 if c<=0.04045 else ((c+0.055)/1.055)**2.4

def oklch(L,C,h):
    L/=100; h=math.radians(h); a=C*math.cos(h); b=C*math.sin(h)
    l_=L+0.3963377774*a+0.2158037573*b; m_=L-0.1055613458*a-0.0638541728*b; s_=L-0.0894841775*a-1.2914855480*b
    l=l_**3; m=m_**3; s=s_**3
    return tuple(lin_to_srgb(x) for x in (4.0767416621*l-3.3077115913*m+0.2309699292*s, -1.2684380046*l+2.6097574011*m-0.3413193965*s, -0.0041960863*l-0.7034186147*m+1.7076147010*s))

def lum(rgb):
    r,g,b=[srgb_to_linear(c) for c in rgb]; return .2126*r+.7152*g+.0722*b

def cr(a,b):
    x,y=lum(a),lum(b); return (max(x,y)+.05)/(min(x,y)+.05)

def mix(a,p,b): return tuple(a[i]*p+b[i]*(1-p) for i in range(3))
def hx(rgb): return '#' + ''.join(f'{round(c*255):02x}' for c in rgb)
raised=oklch(96,.01,70); border=oklch(85,.01,70)
for L in [60,57,56,55,54,53,52,50]:
    acc=oklch(L,.15,50); b=mix(acc,.70,border)
    print(L,hx(b),f'{cr(b,raised):.2f}')
PY" in /home/huwei/ai_codings/distill-portal

- **Findings**:
  - blocking — `apps/frontend/src/components/Toast.css:107` — `.toast.info` still fails the 3:1 non-text contrast target in light mode. With current `--color-accent: oklch(60% 0.15 50)` and `--color-border: oklch(85% 0.01 70)`, the 70% mix is about 2.79:1 against `--color-surface-raised`, so the preemptive WCAG mitigation did not actually pass.
  - blocking — `apps/frontend/src/styles/tokens.css:66`, `apps/frontend/src/components/ActionBar.css:99`, `apps/frontend/src/components/Toast.css:131` — the new light `--color-accent` is used as normal-size text but only reaches about 3.93:1 on `--color-surface` and 3.71:1 on `--color-surface-raised`. That regresses AA for `.action-bar-clear` and `.toast-details summary`.
  - blocking — `apps/frontend/src/styles/tokens.css:65` and `apps/frontend/src/styles/tokens.css:187` — `--color-border-strong` does not meet the documented 3:1 SC 1.4.11 target against `--color-surface` in either scheme. Current values are about 2.53:1 light and 2.13:1 dark, yet this token is used for actual control boundaries.

- **Missing Evidence**: none

- **Required Changes**:
  - Change light `--color-accent` to a passing value, e.g. `oklch(55% 0.15 50)`, then remeasure `.toast.info`, `.action-bar-clear`, and `.toast-details summary`.
  - Change `--color-border-strong` to passing values, e.g. light `oklch(65% 0.02 70)` and dark `oklch(48% 0.02 70)`, then remeasure control-boundary contrast.
  - Update any token comments / WCAG notes that still cite the old ratios, and rerun `bun run build` plus the hex-count check.

- **Notes**:
  - Verified: no `.ts` / `.tsx` changes, protected paths clean, cascade order intact, build parses, hex count remains 24.
  - Fraunces two-file deviation looks acceptable: both WOFF2 files exist and are each under 80 KB.
